# Runbook progress — the tracker

**What this is.** The single place that says where the Redkiln-RS runbook stands: which stage is live, which decisions are
recorded, which of the audit's 110 concerns are closed, and what the validators last said. `runbook/INDEX.md` is the map;
this file is the odometer. Update it in the same commit as the work it records; a row that says `done` without a commit
hash beside it is a claim, not a record.

**Legend.** Stage status: `not started` · `in progress` · `checkpoint passed <hash>` · `halted (Halt N)`. PIA disposition:
`open` · `done` (edit landed) · `recorded` (decision landed as ADR/DECISIONS) · `awaiting-ryan` · `deferred (<reason>)` ·
`not-applicable (<reason>)`. Every non-`open` row names where it landed.

---

🛑 **No erratum was written this pass, and three owed ones are withdrawn AS INSTRUMENTS.** Under
**`DECISIONS-2026-09-15 D15-2`** a criterion correction takes a **dated in-place register amendment**, because a
criterion in an amendable register is not a frozen record — `ERRATA.md`:273-276 concedes the route in its own words
and `s7b` §7b.11 precondition 2 asks for exactly that and names no erratum at all. **`E-0071-1`**, **`E-0079-2`** and
**`E-0105-1`** are withdrawn **as instruments and not as obligations**: each correction still lands, in place and
dated. The correction owed against **AC-484**'s third instrument takes the same form. **Nothing was written to
`references/adr/ERRATA.md`, its convention header is unchanged, and `E-0520-1` and every existing erratum are
untouched.** Every site that said an erratum was owed is re-pointed — including §1's row 7b above and golden
constraint 1 in `00-preamble.md`, whose stale *"ADR-0079 is `proposed`"* label is now a dated in-place correction.

**Six residuals this pass did not settle**, named at `spikes/DECISIONS-2026-09-15.md` so nobody rediscovers them:

- **R-1** — **AC-389's second THEN half (*"the attempt is appended as an event"*) has no event kind to be appended
  as.** `EventKind` is closed at 29, ADR-0112's eleven `StepError` variants carry no member for a refused appetite
  raise, and design §5's table has no row; the crate says so in `crates/rk-kernel/tests/it/budget.rs`’s own module doc, under its heading *"AND AC-389's OTHER HALF IS STILL UNBUILT AND STILL OWED A RECORD"* — **cited by that heading and checkable with `grep -cF`, never by line**: the line range carried here from `spikes/DECISIONS-2026-09-15.md` R-1 lands in AC-022’s fan-out assertions instead, and the single line number that replaced it was wrong twice over — it named one site for a phrase that occurs at two. The **29** and the **eleven** are `rk-types`’ and ADR-0112’s to own and are not re-derived here.
- **R-2** — **ADR-0122 decision 5(c)**, the one lettered item of five that did not land.
- **R-3** — **§4's scope note**, *"Everything in the criteria set is configuration-time and spends no dispatch"*, is imprecise once AC-532
  lands in that area. Named by **D15-3** and deliberately not edited by it.
- **R-4** — ~~**AC-294's third instrument is NOT built.**~~ **PARTLY discharged 2026-09-15, in the same
  session that raised it, and STILL OPEN on its other half** — `spikes/DECISIONS-2026-09-15.md`’s own R-4 row
  says exactly that, having corrected a first wording it describes as *"flatly, which overstated it against the
  register's own more careful wording"*. The module-boundary lint was built test-first as
  `crates/rk-kernel/src/module_boundary.rs` with `crates/rk-kernel/tests/it/module_boundary.rs`
  (`ac294_run_state_construction_sites_are_module_bounded`), and it **scans code lines only and proves it**: a
  comment-only control carrying every banned spelling must yield zero violations. That covers the **`--stay`
  half** ONLY; the **block-value half is ARMED, NOT DISCHARGED, and R-4 stays open on it** — that half names
  construction sites outside `rk-kernel::policy`, and this workspace has no `policy` module and no block-value
  type, so there is nothing for the rule to scan — and **AC-294’s dated amendment in the register is the
  authority on that half; no count of it is restated here.** Checkpoint 8's new line asserts the **case file**, not the lint.
- **R-5** — **a record routing `ActivitySpec` assembly.** D-11 settled the *build scope* (7c); the record is owed.
- **R-6** — **`references/adr/README.md` is stale against ADR-0122 in five places and indexes no row for it**
  (:3, :5, :73, :269, :281). Pre-existing; an index amendable in place, not an erratum.

| **Step 6.1 — executed (2026-09-13)** | — | **The three S-GIX arms ran at the pin, before any worktree code, and ADR-0091 was written from their results.** `cargo test -p rk-git -- --ignored s_gix_` selects **exactly 3** and exits 0. **`S-GIX/a: go`** — oracle and candidate both `<main>/.git`; gix answers the unresolved traversal `<main>/.git/worktrees/linked/../..`, which `realpath` and **inode identity** confirm is the same directory, measured independently of the comparator under test. **`S-GIX/b: no-go`** on its FIRST conjunct: over the resolved source trees, `gix` **0** occurrences of `trailer`, `gix-actor` **0**, `gix-object` **141** all read-side in `src/commit/message/{body,mod}.rs`; every public item is a read (`Trailers`, `TrailerRef`, `BodyRef::trailers()`, the attribution helpers) and `without_trailer()` returns `&BStr`. b2 therefore ran against the hand-rolled route only: **3 of 5** byte-identical, `divider` first differing at byte 14 and `crlf` at byte 29. **`S-GIX/c: go`** — both HEAD trees `53819b7e0cdf98c12e7dee641f57f9a13c30a1f2`, at a SHA one commit behind the tip, over a `file://` URL, with the git-side clone asserted genuinely shallow first. |

The comparator, not the arm, was wrong: it compared spellings where it is documented to absorb them. This is not a defect and the greps are not redundant: each is a FLOOR against a shape the compiler would accept (a comment-only occurrence, a renamed field, a deleted assertion), which is exactly what F1 and F4 then falsified. **But it means "I falsified the block" and "I falsified this line" are different claims**, and only the second is evidence about the line. Read with ADR-0056, ADR-0073 and ADR-0022 there is none: lowest `method_rank`, equal rank is a **load-time** error, and the mixed-toolchain case is resolved at step 2 by keyed expansion. **No record was owed and none was written.** **Mislabelling runs both ways and both are expensive:** a false FORCED lets a stage settle a record's ambiguity in code (which is exactly what L4 had already done), and a false FREE sends the operator a decision the record already made. This is the same family as the 7a defect where two agents shared a working tree, reached from the sequential direction rather than the concurrent one: **a phase boundary is not a guarantee that the previous phase left the tree usable.** The fix phase repaired the module and every gate is green, but the three verdicts from that run are **low-confidence by construction** and were treated as such. **The repair is a green-gate between build and review** — a phase that fails the build must not hand a tree to a reviewer — and it is owed to the next workflow, not to a file. *(And the caller's first diagnosis was wrong: "a test written into `src/`". `replay.rs` is a legitimate runtime-boundary module — it opens files, and `crate::select` denies that — so the fix was to repair the two bugs, not to move the file.)* |

N passed` grep (it matches any binary in a workspace run and so does not attest the bundle was read), and the `Command::new("git")` line (shape (g), an absence-of-output pass condition satisfied by a workspace with no git exec at all — which is why the wrapper spells `Command::new("git")` as a **literal** rather than binding a resolved path). A behavioural test cannot pin cwd without mutating state shared by every other test in the binary, so the structural grep is the instrument for that half. (2) *Ignore the store's worktree row and authorise on the branch alone* → caught by **the behavioural test and nothing else**; every other test stayed green, **including the foreign-worktree refusal**, which passed by accident because that fixture is also on another branch. The parent re-execs the test binary as a child writer; both write 40 transactions × 40 rows × 4 KiB as **different items**; both complete with **zero lock errors**, all 3,200 rows present, **one** store file. **And it discriminates**: with `busy_timeout = 0` the test fails **3 of 3 runs** with the parent hitting `database is locked`, and at 5000 ms it passes. | **The two owed errata, discharged — and one of them was not an erratum (2026-09-13)** | Two were recorded as OWED in code comments: against **ADR-0084**'s `bundled`-on-"the adapter" wording (`crates/rk-store/Cargo.toml`) and against **ADR-0100 decision 4**'s parenthetical (`deny.toml`) | **One was written; the other was withdrawn as unwarranted, and saying so is the finding.** **E-0100-1 is written** (ERRATA.md; README's `0100` row marked `(errata)`): ADR-0100 decision 4's parenthetical — *"equivalently `cargo deny check advisories` with `unmaintained = \"deny\"`"* — **does not parse** at cargo-deny 0.20.2, which changed that key from a lint **level** to a **scope**: `error[unexpected-value]: expected '["all", "workspace", "transitive", "none"]'`. Re-measured 2026-09-13 in an **isolated probe crate**, so the result is a property of the tool and not of this workspace's graph; `unmaintained = "all"` then reports `advisories ok`. The Decision's normative sentence, its consequence (*"a red build, not a silence"*) and its forbids bullet all stand — only the claim of equivalence is withdrawn. **ADR-0084 needed no erratum.** Read against the record rather than against my own earlier comment: 0084's Decision says *"the EventStore adapter opens with the `bundled` feature"*, which it still will; **nothing says only the adapter may declare it**, and **no forbids bullet in ADR-0084 or ADR-0100 scopes `rusqlite` to a crate** — unlike the YAML rule, which does exactly that for `redkiln-migrate`. Cargo feature unification makes the resolved graph and the built artifact identical whichever member declares it. What **was** contradicted is **design §9.2:1732**, whose crate column read `rk-adapter-sqlite` **only** — the design, not a frozen record, and amended with a dated note. The over-claiming comment in `crates/rk-store/Cargo.toml` is corrected in place. |
| **Where an erratum may reach, settled on precedent rather than on reading (2026-09-13)** | Both sentences sit in `## Decision`, and ADR-0092 says a correction that *moves* a Decision sentence *"is a supersession and takes a new record"* | The question is whether an erratum may touch a line **inside** `## Decision` at all. Measured against the existing set rather than argued: of the 34 lines the errata cite, **two are Decision lines** — ADR-0067:48 and ADR-0086:55 — and **E-0086-1** is squarely on point, withdrawing *"Line 55's instruction to propagate the versions"* while closing *"Decision unchanged — the four crates are excluded on ADR-0003's grounds, which do not depend on version currency."* So the convention in practice is ADR-0092's literal test: does **every sentence of `## Decision` still stand**? Not: does the correction fall inside that section? For E-0100-1 the normative sentence, its consequence and its forbids bullet all stand, so an erratum is right and a supersession would be an over-reaction. The precedent is cited inside the entry so the next reader does not have to re-derive it. |
